## 2026-11-06 — Original OpenXR LOCAL unknown changeTime quarantined until actual XR restart (new exact SHA verified source)

- **New independent real-source defect:** The already implemented `XR_TYPE_EVENT_DATA_REFERENCE_SPACE_CHANGE_PENDING` origin-rebase path had an unguarded invalid `changeTime` condition: a zero/nonpositive timestamp was accepted as a pending event, then an arbitrary positive predicted frame was treated as the true transition. An invalid second timestamp after a pending known change could also be silently ignored. Unlike already fixed `XR_TYPE_EVENT_DATA_EVENTS_LOST`, this is an explicitly delivered but untrustworthy event-time problem.
- **Actual new x64→x86 fix:** `src/common/model2_reference_space_epoch.hpp` now records a separate non-recoverable unknown-time state and refuses every subsequent old or new LOCAL predicted frame, even if a later positive timestamp arrives. Only a real `XR_SESSION_STATE_READY` / `BeginSession` advances the generation and clears quarantine. The real `host/xr_frame.cpp` revokes original Win32 pose IPC, cached stereo/original mono and exact x64 eye history, reports `openxr_local_change_time_unknown` in F9 capture, and does not falsely log a successful new-LOCAL event. The original D3D9 physical game Draw, Present, input and FFB, typed x86/x64 IPC layout and pass classification remain unchanged.
- **Native regressions:** `tests/model2_reference_space_epoch_test.cpp` checks zero, negative, known-then-unknown, untrusted future timestamps, later valid event not healing uncertainty, and session recovery. `tests/model2_reference_space_source_chain_test.cpp` connects the quarantine to actual pose-history invalidation and old physical Present ticket rejection, then real-gate restart behavior, built for Win32 original probe and Win64 OpenXR native tests.

## 2026-11-06 — Real XR LOCAL origin recenter and loss events → coherent Win64 pose generation → original x86 CPU-world eye recenter (new source change)

- **New source defect addressed:** the real Win64 OpenXR event loop handled HMD session/instance loss but did not handle `XR_TYPE_EVENT_DATA_REFERENCE_SPACE_CHANGE_PENDING`. OpenXR 1.1.65 defines the LOCAL-space origin transition at `changeTime`. A user/runtime recenter could leave original x86 CPU-world `Model2HeadRecenter` anchored to an obsolete LOCAL origin while the Win64 XR host accepted newer tracked `xrLocateViews` as if unchanged. Previously published original D3D9 GameStereo and original mono/menu world-fixed quad could cross this source-space transition.
- **Headset event loss and on-device diagnostics:** `ReferenceSpaceEpochGate::NotifyEventsLost` fail-closes every future frame until a real XR session restart. An events-lost notification can conceal a FUTURE LOCAL change whose effective `changeTime` is unknowable; immediately resuming in a guessed coordinate origin would incorrectly mix source frames later. No synthetic assumption about timing is accepted. The Win64 F9 local wizard log exposes only `openxr_local_reference_epoch` and `openxr_local_reference_pending`; the original Win32 CPU-world renderer logs a pointer-free `model2_vr_cpu_world_local_recenter` event when the actual frame rebases to a new source generation. No original game coordinates, pixels or HMD tracking pose exported.
- **Atomic publisher contract:** `src/common/stereo_pose_publication_gate.hpp` also rejects a zero/unknown reference-space epoch instead of publishing a valid-but-unversioned HMD pose to the original Win32 game. The existing Windows x86/x64 publication tests now cover that rejection, while the legacy shared IPC binary layout and diagnostic/mono transport remain unchanged.
- **Actual connected implementation:** `src/common/model2_reference_space_epoch.hpp` is the monotonic bounded source-generation gate; the real `host/xr_frame.cpp` handles LOCAL change and `EVENTS_LOST`, invalidates pose IPC, cached original game eye frames, OpenXR pose history, menu quad, and pending XR uploads immediately; quarantines every predicted XR frame before the specified `changeTime`; drops the transition frame; then publishes the first tracked new-LOCAL `xrLocateViews` pair tagged with the next nonzero generation in the existing `pose::Frame.reserved` field (size, x86/x64 ABI and version unchanged). SESSION READY also starts a new generation; exhausted generation fails closed without wrapping.
- **Real original x86 D3D9 physical Present epoch fence:** `src/probe/daytona_source_pose_commit_gate.hpp` now includes the latched XR LOCAL reference generation in the original game Draw pose ticket. Both GPU-eye `daytona_native_stereo.cpp` and CPU-world `verified_world_bridge.cpp` pin the actual source generation and refuse a later physical Present after XR LOCAL changed, even if the new pose is otherwise valid/fresh and sequence-near. This rejects cross-origin original game eyes before Win64 compositor history checks. Test `daytona_source_pose_commit_gate_test.cpp` adds exact epoch switch/legacy negative cases, retaining the original game Draw/Present/HUD/FFB.
- **Original Win32 CPU-world route:** `src/vr/model2_head_recenter.hpp` now atomically reanchors the source HMD origin when an independently authenticated CPU-world frame carries a newer LOCAL epoch, rejects any old/legacy epoch after that transition, preserves all original game-view calibration and the real D3D9 Draw/Scene/Present/FFB paths. `tests/model2_reference_space_epoch_test.cpp` exercises exact future boundary, repeated/missing LOCAL changes and session restart on both Win32 and Win64; `tests/model2_head_recenter_test.cpp` covers right/left physical eye reanchoring, stale epoch rejection and invalid-frame atomicity.
- **Actual connected native chain regression:** `tests/model2_reference_space_source_chain_test.cpp` now exercises host XR source publish → host exact view history → original x86 physical Present source ticket → independently calibrated CPU-world origin rebasing on the real ABI-tagged LOCAL epoch, old source rejection at the true changeTime, unknown-event-loss hold and session-restart recovery. Built in both Win32 probe CTest and independent Win64 OpenXR host CTest; synthetic test data does not grant World3D or claim real Quest/Daytona runtime proof.

## Scope and SSOT

- Primary public implementation repository: `thp32tt/Model2VR-tools@main` (HEAD is SSOT).

- Main test target: Daytona USA on ElSemi Model 2 Emulator, 32-bit D3D9 process bridged to 64-bit OpenXR host. Preserve Force Feedback/steering and normal 2D fallback.
- This is not the OutRun 2006 VR project and not the Uncharted Waters II web port. No OutRun repo, DX11 conversion branch or UW2 development is to be modified as part of this lane.

## Continuous development execution contract

- The C-lane automated message after initial bootstrap is exactly: `모델2 VR 개발 계속진행해`.
- One command is an extended **real source implementation session**: target 20+ minutes of productive multi-function development, not 1 minute of implementation and 3 minutes of checking. ChatGPT platform/model limits can end an individual response earlier; NEVER idle/sleep/loop to falsely claim 20 minutes.
- Resume from the latest incomplete source/host/emulator integration, not from a brand-new microtask/task-ID per message. For each response, follow as much of the actual D3D9 draw/state/resource -> x86 bridge -> x64 OpenXR stereo -> eye composition -> launch/recovery path as possible. Work through multiple adjacent features, actual bugs and tests; one tiny CI-green commit is progress, not a terminal condition.
- Target roughly 70%+ of meaningful effort on source implementation, at most 30% on proportional verification/evidence/reporting. Never skip required checks or claim tests passed when not run. Prefer one consolidated test/CI pass per cohesive implementation. Do not re-run CI for the exact same SHA.
- A production build should produce a usable direct-launch Windows batch file and diagnostic log on failure, not demand repeated duplicate probes. Existing user feedback: launcher briefly flashes and opens 2D; preserve logs and diagnose genuine OpenXR host connection, swapchain/eye rendering, game/process startup and Direct3D hook, without inventing evidence.
- Source / exact SHA CI success is NOT HMD visual confirmation. Mark hardware validation UNTESTED until the user actually runs on Quest 3 / PC. Preserve game compatibility and optional hook modes.
